Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… things have just been getting hard to keep up with. At work, my hands feel like they’re moving through glue or something. Like sectioning hair, mixing color—stuff I used to do without thinking—it takes me forever now.

And it’s embarrassing because people are noticing. One of my coworkers made this little joke about me being “extra careful,” and I laughed, but I was like… no, I’m just slow now. Sorry, I know that’s not a very clear answer. That’s mostly why I came in.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s not just work, no. Work is where it’s most obvious because, like, if I mess up a toner bowl or forget the order of something basic, everyone sees it. My manager doesn’t really trust me the same now, and some clients have started acting nervous or asking for someone else, which… yeah, that feels awful.

But it’s outside work too. I keep canceling plans with friends, or I just don’t answer because I feel like I can’t handle showing up. I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I was pretty reliable with that, actually—there was this one lady who always wanted her bangs trimmed the same exact way—but I stopped going. I just kind of pulled away from all of it.

Like sectioning hair, mixing color—stuff I used to do without thinking—it takes me forever now.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because people are noticing. One of my coworkers made this little joke about me being “extra careful,” and I laughed, but I was like… no, I’m just slow now. Sorry, I know that’s not a very clear answer. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these difficulties just at work or in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: It’s not just work, no. Work is where it’s most obvious because, like, if I mess up a toner bowl or forget the order of something basic, everyone sees it. I don’t know if I’d call it fear exactly. I mean, I get tense thinking about it, but then sometimes I think maybe everybody gets tense before work, especially if they have a client who’s picky about highlights or whatever.

I avoid people more. Maybe tiny things, but it’s kind of hard to feel it the way I used to. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… things have just been getting hard to keep up with. At work, my hands feel like they’re moving through glue or something. Like sectioning hair, mixing color—stuff I used to do without thinking—it takes me forever now.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because people are noticing. One of my coworkers made this little joke about me being “extra careful,” and I laughed, but I was like… no, I’m just slow now. Sorry, I know that’s not a very clear answer. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these difficulties just at work or in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: It’s not just work, no. Work is where it’s most obvious because, like, if I mess up a toner bowl or forget the order of something basic, everyone sees it. My manager doesn’t really trust me the same now, and some clients have started acting nervous or asking for someone else, which… yeah, that feels awful.\n\nBut it’s outside work too. I keep canceling plans with friends, or I just don’t answer because I feel like I can’t handle showing up. I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I was pretty reliable with that, actually—there was this one lady who always wanted her bangs trimmed the same exact way—but I stopped going. I just kind of pulled away from all of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you think about performing these tasks at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it fear exactly. I mean, I get tense thinking about it, but then sometimes I think maybe everybody gets tense before work, especially if they have a client who’s picky about highlights or whatever.\n\nIt’s more like… I start thinking about all the little steps, and then my brain just kind of wanders off. Like I’ll remember this one client who used to bring in coffee and talk about her dog the whole time—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not totally sure what the feeling is. Maybe nervous, but I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness sitting in my chest. Like before I even get out of bed, I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I thought I was building?\n\nAnd then I’ll think about stupid little things, like the apartment I wanted someday with nice lighting for doing hair, or this salon chair I saw online once—sorry, that’s not really… yeah. I do feel down most of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to actually enjoy being at the salon, like talking with clients while I was doing their hair, joking around, helping the newer stylists figure out a color mix or whatever. I avoid people more. It’s like I don’t have that spark in me anymore, even for things I used to like.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain situations or activities because you fear they might lead to failure or embarrassment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s because I’m scared of failing or being embarrassed exactly. Like the spark just isn’t there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Maybe tiny things, but it’s kind of hard to feel it the way I used to. Work is where it’s most obvious because, like, if I mess up a toner bowl or forget the order of something basic, everyone sees it. I keep canceling plans with friends, or I just don’t answer because I feel like I can’t handle showing up. I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I was pretty reliable with that, actually—there was this one lady who always wanted her bangs trimmed the same exact way—but I stopped going. Not much, honestly. I know I kind of said it before, but… I used to like the salon stuff, like talking with clients, hearing all their stories, or helping the newer girls when they were unsure about something. That used to feel good.

One of my coworkers made this little joke about me being “extra careful,” and I laughed, but I was like… no, I’m just slow now. Sorry, I know that’s not a very clear answer. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these difficulties just at work or in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: It’s not just work, no. Work is where it’s most obvious because, like, if I mess up a toner bowl or forget the order of something basic, everyone sees it. I keep canceling plans with friends, or I just don’t answer because I feel like I can’t handle showing up. I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I was pretty reliable with that, actually—there was this one lady who always wanted her bangs trimmed the same exact way—but I stopped going. I just kind of pulled away from all of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you think about performing these tasks at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it fear exactly. I mean, I get tense thinking about it, but then sometimes I think maybe everybody gets tense before work, especially if they have a client who’s picky about highlights or whatever.\n\nIt’s more like… I start thinking about all the little steps, and then my brain just kind of wanders off. Like I’ll remember this one client who used to bring in coffee and talk about her dog the whole time—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not totally sure what the feeling is. Maybe nervous, but I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness sitting in my chest. Like before I even get out of bed, I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I thought I was building?\n\nAnd then I’ll think about stupid little things, like the apartment I wanted someday with nice lighting for doing hair, or this salon chair I saw online once—sorry, that’s not really… yeah. I do feel down most of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to actually enjoy being at the salon, like talking with clients while I was doing their hair, joking around, helping the newer stylists figure out a color mix or whatever. I avoid people more. It’s like I don’t have that spark in me anymore, even for things I used to like.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain situations or activities because you fear they might lead to failure or embarrassment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s because I’m scared of failing or being embarrassed exactly. It’s more just… I pull back, but I don’t always know what the reason is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot. More severe, I think. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because some days I still get up and do what I’m supposed to do… but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.

It’s like before I even brush my teeth I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I was planning? Which sounds awful out loud. I used to have these ideas about where I’d work, maybe having my own chair, my own little space, and now it just feels… far away. So yeah, not mild. More like it’s sitting on me most of the time.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… things have just been getting hard to keep up with. At work, my hands feel like they’re moving through glue or something. Like sectioning hair, mixing color—stuff I used to do without thinking—it takes me forever now.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because people are noticing. One of my coworkers made this little joke about me being “extra careful,” and I laughed, but I was like… no, I’m just slow now. Sorry, I know that’s not a very clear answer. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these difficulties just at work or in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: It’s not just work, no. Work is where it’s most obvious because, like, if I mess up a toner bowl or forget the order of something basic, everyone sees it. My manager doesn’t really trust me the same now, and some clients have started acting nervous or asking for someone else, which… yeah, that feels awful.\n\nBut it’s outside work too. I keep canceling plans with friends, or I just don’t answer because I feel like I can’t handle showing up. I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I was pretty reliable with that, actually—there was this one lady who always wanted her bangs trimmed the same exact way—but I stopped going. I just kind of pulled away from all of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you think about performing these tasks at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it fear exactly. I mean, I get tense thinking about it, but then sometimes I think maybe everybody gets tense before work, especially if they have a client who’s picky about highlights or whatever.\n\nIt’s more like… I start thinking about all the little steps, and then my brain just kind of wanders off. Like I’ll remember this one client who used to bring in coffee and talk about her dog the whole time—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not totally sure what the feeling is. Maybe nervous, but I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness sitting in my chest. Like before I even get out of bed, I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I thought I was building?\n\nAnd then I’ll think about stupid little things, like the apartment I wanted someday with nice lighting for doing hair, or this salon chair I saw online once—sorry, that’s not really… yeah. I do feel down most of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to actually enjoy being at the salon, like talking with clients while I was doing their hair, joking around, helping the newer stylists figure out a color mix or whatever. And I used to hike with friends sometimes—there was this one trail with these weird little painted rocks people left, I don’t know why I remember that.\n\nBut now I mostly just keep my head down. I avoid people more. It’s like I don’t have that spark in me anymore, even for things I used to like.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain situations or activities because you fear they might lead to failure or embarrassment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s because I’m scared of failing or being embarrassed exactly. I do avoid some things, but it’s hard to tell why in the moment.\n\nLike with work stuff, sometimes I’ll think about messing up someone’s hair, and then I start thinking about this one time in school when we had to practice foils and mine came out uneven—well, not terrible, but I remember the instructor making this face, and then I’m thinking about faces people make, and… sorry.\n\nI guess I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern where I’m avoiding things specifically because I’m afraid of embarrassment. It’s more just… I pull back, but I don’t always know what the reason is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot. I keep getting stuck on the thought that I’m just kind of a burden on everyone. Like people have to work around me or clean up after me emotionally, I guess.\n\nAnd then I think maybe I deserve all this because I couldn’t keep basic things together—my money, my job stuff, even salon responsibilities that should’ve been simple. Like remembering what color bowls were for what client, or keeping my station right, and then I start thinking about how some stylists always have those little carts perfectly organized with the clips all lined up… sorry. But yeah, I feel guilty about it a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I mean… I used to get something out of chatting with clients, helping the newer girls at the salon, even hiking with friends sometimes. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to be around people too much.\n\nSometimes I’ll think maybe I should go for a walk or text someone, but then it feels like too much. Like the spark just isn’t there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Maybe tiny things, but it’s kind of hard to feel it the way I used to. Like I used to really enjoy talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they were learning something, and going hiking with friends… even just the way the air smells on certain trails, you know, like pine and dirt after rain.\n\nBut now I mostly keep my head down. I avoid people more, not because I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. So if something feels nice, it’s really small and it doesn’t last long.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a long time. Like, I keep trying to think of when it started exactly, because it kind of blended in with everything else, but… about a year and two months, I think.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember around then I kept telling myself it was just a bad week, or maybe I was tired from work, and then weeks turned into months. But yeah… the heavy, flat feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in the way I used to. I used to look forward to chatting with clients, like hearing about their kids or their vacations, and helping the newer stylists when they were nervous about a cut or color. That used to make me feel useful, I guess.\n\nAnd hiking with friends too… I used to actually plan for that, like pick a trail and pack snacks, and now even thinking about texting someone back feels like a whole thing. Mostly I just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more now, not because they did anything, I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. So if something feels nice, it’s really small and it doesn’t last long.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a long time. Like, I keep trying to think of when it started exactly, because it kind of blended in with everything else, but… about a year and two months, I think.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember around then I kept telling myself it was just a bad week, or maybe I was tired from work, and then weeks turned into months. But yeah… the heavy, flat feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in the way I used to. Mostly I just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more now, not because they did anything, I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. I don’t really notice it happening at the time, but I’ll get to nighttime and realize I basically just had coffee and, like, a few bites of something.\n\nIt’s weird because at the salon I used to snack between clients, or someone would bring muffins or whatever, and now I just kind of forget. Then I’m changing clothes and they feel looser, like my jeans don’t sit the same. So… yeah, I guess I’ve been skipping meals without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. One of my coworkers made this little joke about me being “extra careful,” and I laughed, but I was like… no, I’m just slow now. Sorry, I know that’s not a very clear answer. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these difficulties just at work or in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: It’s not just work, no. Work is where it’s most obvious because, like, if I mess up a toner bowl or forget the order of something basic, everyone sees it. My manager doesn’t really trust me the same now, and some clients have started acting nervous or asking for someone else, which… yeah, that feels awful.\n\nBut it’s outside work too. I keep canceling plans with friends, or I just don’t answer because I feel like I can’t handle showing up. I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I was pretty reliable with that, actually—there was this one lady who always wanted her bangs trimmed the same exact way—but I stopped going. I just kind of pulled away from all of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you think about performing these tasks at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it fear exactly. I mean, I get tense thinking about it, but then sometimes I think maybe everybody gets tense before work, especially if they have a client who’s picky about highlights or whatever.\n\nIt’s more like… I start thinking about all the little steps, and then my brain just kind of wanders off. Like I’ll remember this one client who used to bring in coffee and talk about her dog the whole time—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not totally sure what the feeling is. Maybe nervous, but I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness sitting in my chest. Like before I even get out of bed, I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I thought I was building?\n\nAnd then I’ll think about stupid little things, like the apartment I wanted someday with nice lighting for doing hair, or this salon chair I saw online once—sorry, that’s not really… yeah. I do feel down most of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to actually enjoy being at the salon, like talking with clients while I was doing their hair, joking around, helping the newer stylists figure out a color mix or whatever. And I used to hike with friends sometimes—there was this one trail with these weird little painted rocks people left, I don’t know why I remember that.\n\nBut now I mostly just keep my head down. I avoid people more. It’s like I don’t have that spark in me anymore, even for things I used to like.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain situations or activities because you fear they might lead to failure or embarrassment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s because I’m scared of failing or being embarrassed exactly. I do avoid some things, but it’s hard to tell why in the moment.\n\nLike with work stuff, sometimes I’ll think about messing up someone’s hair, and then I start thinking about this one time in school when we had to practice foils and mine came out uneven—well, not terrible, but I remember the instructor making this face, and then I’m thinking about faces people make, and… sorry.\n\nI guess I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern where I’m avoiding things specifically because I’m afraid of embarrassment. It’s more just… I pull back, but I don’t always know what the reason is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot. I keep getting stuck on the thought that I’m just kind of a burden on everyone. Like people have to work around me or clean up after me emotionally, I guess.\n\nAnd then I think maybe I deserve all this because I couldn’t keep basic things together—my money, my job stuff, even salon responsibilities that should’ve been simple. Like remembering what color bowls were for what client, or keeping my station right, and then I start thinking about how some stylists always have those little carts perfectly organized with the clips all lined up… sorry. But yeah, I feel guilty about it a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I mean… I used to get something out of chatting with clients, helping the newer girls at the salon, even hiking with friends sometimes. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to be around people too much.\n\nSometimes I’ll think maybe I should go for a walk or text someone, but then it feels like too much. Like the spark just isn’t there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Maybe tiny things, but it’s kind of hard to feel it the way I used to. Like I used to really enjoy talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they were learning something, and going hiking with friends… even just the way the air smells on certain trails, you know, like pine and dirt after rain.\n\nBut now I mostly keep my head down. I avoid people more, not because I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. So if something feels nice, it’s really small and it doesn’t last long.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a long time. Like, I keep trying to think of when it started exactly, because it kind of blended in with everything else, but… about a year and two months, I think.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember around then I kept telling myself it was just a bad week, or maybe I was tired from work, and then weeks turned into months. But yeah… the heavy, flat feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in the way I used to. I used to look forward to chatting with clients, like hearing about their kids or their vacations, and helping the newer stylists when they were nervous about a cut or color. That used to make me feel useful, I guess.\n\nAnd hiking with friends too… I used to actually plan for that, like pick a trail and pack snacks, and now even thinking about texting someone back feels like a whole thing. Mostly I just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more now, not because they did anything, I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. I don’t really notice it happening at the time, but I’ll get to nighttime and realize I basically just had coffee and, like, a few bites of something.\n\nIt’s weird because at the salon I used to snack between clients, or someone would bring muffins or whatever, and now I just kind of forget. Then I’m changing clothes and they feel looser, like my jeans don’t sit the same. So… yeah, I guess I’ve been skipping meals without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I know I kind of said it before, but… I used to like the salon stuff, like talking with clients, hearing all their stories, or helping the newer girls when they were unsure about something. That used to feel good.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends, too. We had this one trail we’d do where someone always forgot water, which was usually me, actually… but now I don’t really look forward to it. I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your sadness is mild or more severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: More severe, I think. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because some days I still get up and do what I’m supposed to do… but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like before I even brush my teeth I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I was planning? Which sounds awful out loud. I used to have these ideas about where I’d work, maybe having my own chair, my own little space, and now it just feels… far away. So yeah, not mild. More like it’s sitting on me most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have lost interest in most things or just some things?\n\nPatient: Most things, I think… yeah. Like, I can’t really think of much I actually look forward to anymore.\n\nI used to be really into chatting with clients at the salon, you know, like hearing about their kids or their vacations or whatever drama was happening. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to get pulled into conversations.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends used to be a whole thing too, like we’d make playlists and stop for snacks after… actually there was this one place with the best fries, but… yeah, even that doesn’t feel fun now. I mean… I used to get something out of chatting with clients, helping the newer girls at the salon, even hiking with friends sometimes. Like the spark just isn’t there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Maybe tiny things, but it’s kind of hard to feel it the way I used to. Like I used to really enjoy talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they were learning something, and going hiking with friends… even just the way the air smells on certain trails, you know, like pine and dirt after rain.\n\nBut now I mostly keep my head down. I avoid people more, not because I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. So if something feels nice, it’s really small and it doesn’t last long.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a long time. Like, I keep trying to think of when it started exactly, because it kind of blended in with everything else, but… about a year and two months, I think.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember around then I kept telling myself it was just a bad week, or maybe I was tired from work, and then weeks turned into months. But yeah… the heavy, flat feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in the way I used to. I used to look forward to chatting with clients, like hearing about their kids or their vacations, and helping the newer stylists when they were nervous about a cut or color. That used to make me feel useful, I guess.\n\nAnd hiking with friends too… I used to actually plan for that, like pick a trail and pack snacks, and now even thinking about texting someone back feels like a whole thing. Mostly I just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more now, not because they did anything, I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. I don’t really notice it happening at the time, but I’ll get to nighttime and realize I basically just had coffee and, like, a few bites of something.\n\nIt’s weird because at the salon I used to snack between clients, or someone would bring muffins or whatever, and now I just kind of forget. Then I’m changing clothes and they feel looser, like my jeans don’t sit the same. So… yeah, I guess I’ve been skipping meals without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I know I kind of said it before, but… I used to like the salon stuff, like talking with clients, hearing all their stories, or helping the newer girls when they were unsure about something. That used to feel good.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends, too. We had this one trail we’d do where someone always forgot water, which was usually me, actually… but now I don’t really look forward to it. I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your sadness is mild or more severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: More severe, I think. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because some days I still get up and do what I’m supposed to do… but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like before I even brush my teeth I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I was planning? Which sounds awful out loud. I used to have these ideas about where I’d work, maybe having my own chair, my own little space, and now it just feels… far away. So yeah, not mild. More like it’s sitting on me most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have lost interest in most things or just some things?\n\nPatient: Most things, I think… yeah. Like, I can’t really think of much I actually look forward to anymore.\n\nI used to be really into chatting with clients at the salon, you know, like hearing about their kids or their vacations or whatever drama was happening. And I’d help the newer stylists when they got nervous, which made me feel useful. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to get pulled into conversations.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends used to be a whole thing too, like we’d make playlists and stop for snacks after… actually there was this one place with the best fries, but… yeah, even that doesn’t feel fun now. I don’t hate any of it. I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really much, no. I keep trying to think of something because it sounds so sad to say there’s nothing, but the things I used to enjoy don’t really hit the same.\n\nLike at the salon, I used to love talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they got stuck or nervous. There was this one girl who always panicked about bangs—honestly, bangs make everyone panic—but I liked being the person she asked. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to get pulled into stuff.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends used to be fun too, but now even that feels like… effort. I avoid people more. It’s not that I hate them. I just don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I have also pulled away from friends and stopped showing up for volunteer haircuts at the senior center.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… most of the time, I think it is.

Like work has gotten harder in ways that feel embarrassing because it’s basic stuff, you know? Salon routines I used to do without thinking, now I make mistakes or I’m just slow, and then I can tell my manager doesn’t really trust me the same. Some clients too. They used to ask for me and now I feel like they’re watching me mess up before I even do anything.

And I’ve been canceling things I normally handled. Not big dramatic things, just… things people counted on me for. I stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts at the senior center, which makes me feel awful because some of them really looked forward to it. There was this one lady who always wanted her hair curled the exact same way—sorry, that’s not really the point.

I’ve pulled away from my friends too. I don’t answer as much, I don’t go places. So yeah, it’s not just me feeling sad in my room. It’s messing with my actual life.

Yeah… pretty much every day. I mean, maybe it’s not the exact same level all day, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, like this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.

Like before I even get out of bed, I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I thought I was building?\n\nAnd then I’ll think about stupid little things, like the apartment I wanted someday with nice lighting for doing hair, or this salon chair I saw online once—sorry, that’s not really… yeah. I do feel down most of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to actually enjoy being at the salon, like talking with clients while I was doing their hair, joking around, helping the newer stylists figure out a color mix or whatever. And I used to hike with friends sometimes—there was this one trail with these weird little painted rocks people left, I don’t know why I remember that.\n\nBut now I mostly just keep my head down. I avoid people more. It’s like I don’t have that spark in me anymore, even for things I used to like.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain situations or activities because you fear they might lead to failure or embarrassment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s because I’m scared of failing or being embarrassed exactly. I do avoid some things, but it’s hard to tell why in the moment.\n\nLike with work stuff, sometimes I’ll think about messing up someone’s hair, and then I start thinking about this one time in school when we had to practice foils and mine came out uneven—well, not terrible, but I remember the instructor making this face, and then I’m thinking about faces people make, and… sorry.\n\nI guess I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern where I’m avoiding things specifically because I’m afraid of embarrassment. It’s more just… I pull back, but I don’t always know what the reason is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot. I keep getting stuck on the thought that I’m just kind of a burden on everyone. Like people have to work around me or clean up after me emotionally, I guess.\n\nAnd then I think maybe I deserve all this because I couldn’t keep basic things together—my money, my job stuff, even salon responsibilities that should’ve been simple. Like remembering what color bowls were for what client, or keeping my station right, and then I start thinking about how some stylists always have those little carts perfectly organized with the clips all lined up… sorry. But yeah, I feel guilty about it a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I mean… I used to get something out of chatting with clients, helping the newer girls at the salon, even hiking with friends sometimes. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to be around people too much.\n\nSometimes I’ll think maybe I should go for a walk or text someone, but then it feels like too much. Like the spark just isn’t there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Maybe tiny things, but it’s kind of hard to feel it the way I used to. Like I used to really enjoy talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they were learning something, and going hiking with friends… even just the way the air smells on certain trails, you know, like pine and dirt after rain.\n\nBut now I mostly keep my head down. I avoid people more, not because I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. So if something feels nice, it’s really small and it doesn’t last long.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a long time. Like, I keep trying to think of when it started exactly, because it kind of blended in with everything else, but… about a year and two months, I think.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember around then I kept telling myself it was just a bad week, or maybe I was tired from work, and then weeks turned into months. But yeah… the heavy, flat feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in the way I used to. I used to look forward to chatting with clients, like hearing about their kids or their vacations, and helping the newer stylists when they were nervous about a cut or color. That used to make me feel useful, I guess.\n\nAnd hiking with friends too… I used to actually plan for that, like pick a trail and pack snacks, and now even thinking about texting someone back feels like a whole thing. Mostly I just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more now, not because they did anything, I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. I don’t really notice it happening at the time, but I’ll get to nighttime and realize I basically just had coffee and, like, a few bites of something.\n\nIt’s weird because at the salon I used to snack between clients, or someone would bring muffins or whatever, and now I just kind of forget. Then I’m changing clothes and they feel looser, like my jeans don’t sit the same. So… yeah, I guess I’ve been skipping meals without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I know I kind of said it before, but… I used to like the salon stuff, like talking with clients, hearing all their stories, or helping the newer girls when they were unsure about something. That used to feel good.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends, too. We had this one trail we’d do where someone always forgot water, which was usually me, actually… but now I don’t really look forward to it. I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your sadness is mild or more severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: More severe, I think. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because some days I still get up and do what I’m supposed to do… but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like before I even brush my teeth I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I was planning? Which sounds awful out loud. I used to have these ideas about where I’d work, maybe having my own chair, my own little space, and now it just feels… far away. So yeah, not mild. More like it’s sitting on me most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have lost interest in most things or just some things?\n\nPatient: Most things, I think… yeah. Like, I can’t really think of much I actually look forward to anymore.\n\nI used to be really into chatting with clients at the salon, you know, like hearing about their kids or their vacations or whatever drama was happening. And I’d help the newer stylists when they got nervous, which made me feel useful. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to get pulled into conversations.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends used to be a whole thing too, like we’d make playlists and stop for snacks after… actually there was this one place with the best fries, but… yeah, even that doesn’t feel fun now. I don’t hate any of it. I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really much, no. I keep trying to think of something because it sounds so sad to say there’s nothing, but the things I used to enjoy don’t really hit the same.\n\nLike at the salon, I used to love talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they got stuck or nervous. There was this one girl who always panicked about bangs—honestly, bangs make everyone panic—but I liked being the person she asked. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to get pulled into stuff.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends used to be fun too, but now even that feels like… effort. I avoid people more. It’s not that I hate them. I just don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your sadness is interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, I think it is.\n\nLike work has gotten harder in ways that feel embarrassing because it’s basic stuff, you know? Salon routines I used to do without thinking, now I make mistakes or I’m just slow, and then I can tell my manager doesn’t really trust me the same. Some clients too. They used to ask for me and now I feel like they’re watching me mess up before I even do anything.\n\nAnd I’ve been canceling things I normally handled. It’s like I don’t have that spark in me anymore, even for things I used to like.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain situations or activities because you fear they might lead to failure or embarrassment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s because I’m scared of failing or being embarrassed exactly. I do avoid some things, but it’s hard to tell why in the moment.\n\nLike with work stuff, sometimes I’ll think about messing up someone’s hair, and then I start thinking about this one time in school when we had to practice foils and mine came out uneven—well, not terrible, but I remember the instructor making this face, and then I’m thinking about faces people make, and… sorry.\n\nI guess I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern where I’m avoiding things specifically because I’m afraid of embarrassment. It’s more just… I pull back, but I don’t always know what the reason is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot. I keep getting stuck on the thought that I’m just kind of a burden on everyone. Like people have to work around me or clean up after me emotionally, I guess.\n\nAnd then I think maybe I deserve all this because I couldn’t keep basic things together—my money, my job stuff, even salon responsibilities that should’ve been simple. Like remembering what color bowls were for what client, or keeping my station right, and then I start thinking about how some stylists always have those little carts perfectly organized with the clips all lined up… sorry. But yeah, I feel guilty about it a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I mean… I used to get something out of chatting with clients, helping the newer girls at the salon, even hiking with friends sometimes. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to be around people too much.\n\nSometimes I’ll think maybe I should go for a walk or text someone, but then it feels like too much. Like the spark just isn’t there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Maybe tiny things, but it’s kind of hard to feel it the way I used to. Like I used to really enjoy talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they were learning something, and going hiking with friends… even just the way the air smells on certain trails, you know, like pine and dirt after rain.\n\nBut now I mostly keep my head down. I avoid people more, not because I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. So if something feels nice, it’s really small and it doesn’t last long.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a long time. Like, I keep trying to think of when it started exactly, because it kind of blended in with everything else, but… about a year and two months, I think.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember around then I kept telling myself it was just a bad week, or maybe I was tired from work, and then weeks turned into months. But yeah… the heavy, flat feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in the way I used to. I used to look forward to chatting with clients, like hearing about their kids or their vacations, and helping the newer stylists when they were nervous about a cut or color. That used to make me feel useful, I guess.\n\nAnd hiking with friends too… I used to actually plan for that, like pick a trail and pack snacks, and now even thinking about texting someone back feels like a whole thing. Mostly I just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more now, not because they did anything, I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. I don’t really notice it happening at the time, but I’ll get to nighttime and realize I basically just had coffee and, like, a few bites of something.\n\nIt’s weird because at the salon I used to snack between clients, or someone would bring muffins or whatever, and now I just kind of forget. Then I’m changing clothes and they feel looser, like my jeans don’t sit the same. So… yeah, I guess I’ve been skipping meals without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I know I kind of said it before, but… I used to like the salon stuff, like talking with clients, hearing all their stories, or helping the newer girls when they were unsure about something. That used to feel good.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends, too. We had this one trail we’d do where someone always forgot water, which was usually me, actually… but now I don’t really look forward to it. I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your sadness is mild or more severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: More severe, I think. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because some days I still get up and do what I’m supposed to do… but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like before I even brush my teeth I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I was planning? Which sounds awful out loud. I used to have these ideas about where I’d work, maybe having my own chair, my own little space, and now it just feels… far away. So yeah, not mild. More like it’s sitting on me most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have lost interest in most things or just some things?\n\nPatient: Most things, I think… yeah. Like, I can’t really think of much I actually look forward to anymore.\n\nI used to be really into chatting with clients at the salon, you know, like hearing about their kids or their vacations or whatever drama was happening. And I’d help the newer stylists when they got nervous, which made me feel useful. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to get pulled into conversations.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends used to be a whole thing too, like we’d make playlists and stop for snacks after… actually there was this one place with the best fries, but… yeah, even that doesn’t feel fun now. I don’t hate any of it. I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really much, no. I keep trying to think of something because it sounds so sad to say there’s nothing, but the things I used to enjoy don’t really hit the same.\n\nLike at the salon, I used to love talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they got stuck or nervous. There was this one girl who always panicked about bangs—honestly, bangs make everyone panic—but I liked being the person she asked. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to get pulled into stuff.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends used to be fun too, but now even that feels like… effort. I avoid people more. It’s not that I hate them. I just don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your sadness is interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, I think it is.\n\nLike work has gotten harder in ways that feel embarrassing because it’s basic stuff, you know? Salon routines I used to do without thinking, now I make mistakes or I’m just slow, and then I can tell my manager doesn’t really trust me the same. Some clients too. They used to ask for me and now I feel like they’re watching me mess up before I even do anything.\n\nAnd I’ve been canceling things I normally handled. Not big dramatic things, just… things people counted on me for. I stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts at the senior center, which makes me feel awful because some of them really looked forward to it. There was this one lady who always wanted her hair curled the exact same way—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nI’ve pulled away from my friends too. I don’t answer as much, I don’t go places. So yeah, it’s not just me feeling sad in my room. It’s messing with my actual life.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your sadness is present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much every day. I mean, maybe it’s not the exact same level all day, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, like this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll be brushing my teeth or getting dressed for work and I’ll think about how I was supposed to be building this whole life, you know? Like getting better at hair, maybe having my own little chair setup that felt like mine, maybe an apartment with plants I don’t kill—sorry, that’s random. But now it just feels like… what’s the point of trying to build any of it. So yeah, it’s there most days.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your sadness is present most of the day or nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say nearly every day. Most mornings I wake up and it’s just already there, this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt kind of follows me around most of the day too, even if I’m doing normal things. Like I’ll be thinking about work, or how I used to picture building this whole life for myself, and then it just turns into this feeling like… what’s the point of trying. I don’t know. So yeah, it’s not just me feeling sad in my room. It’s messing with my actual life.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your sadness is present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much every day. I mean, maybe it’s not the exact same level all day, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, like this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll be brushing my teeth or getting dressed for work and I’ll think about how I was supposed to be building this whole life, you know? Like getting better at hair, maybe having my own little chair setup that felt like mine, maybe an apartment with plants I don’t kill—sorry, that’s random. But now it just feels like… what’s the point of trying to build any of it. So yeah, it’s there most days.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your sadness is present most of the day or nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say nearly every day. Most mornings I wake up and it’s just already there, this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt kind of follows me around most of the day too, even if I’m doing normal things. Like I’ll be thinking about work, or how I used to picture building this whole life for myself, and then it just turns into this feeling like… what’s the point of trying. I don’t know. It’s hard to explain, but yeah, it’s there a lot.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow many months have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Um… about a year and two months, I think. Like, not just a bad week or something.

It’s strange because I remember thinking last year, “Okay, maybe this is just stress,” and then months kept stacking up. Work is where it’s most obvious because, like, if I mess up a toner bowl or forget the order of something basic, everyone sees it. I do feel down most of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to actually enjoy being at the salon, like talking with clients while I was doing their hair, joking around, helping the newer stylists figure out a color mix or whatever. I avoid people more. It’s like I don’t have that spark in me anymore, even for things I used to like.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain situations or activities because you fear they might lead to failure or embarrassment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s because I’m scared of failing or being embarrassed exactly. I do avoid some things, but it’s hard to tell why in the moment.\n\nLike with work stuff, sometimes I’ll think about messing up someone’s hair, and then I start thinking about this one time in school when we had to practice foils and mine came out uneven—well, not terrible, but I remember the instructor making this face, and then I’m thinking about faces people make, and… sorry.\n\nI guess I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern where I’m avoiding things specifically because I’m afraid of embarrassment. Like I used to really enjoy talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they were learning something, and going hiking with friends… even just the way the air smells on certain trails, you know, like pine and dirt after rain.\n\nBut now I mostly keep my head down. I avoid people more, not because I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. So if something feels nice, it’s really small and it doesn’t last long.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a long time. Like, I keep trying to think of when it started exactly, because it kind of blended in with everything else, but… about a year and two months, I think.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember around then I kept telling myself it was just a bad week, or maybe I was tired from work, and then weeks turned into months. But yeah… the heavy, flat feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in the way I used to. That used to make me feel useful, I guess.\n\nAnd hiking with friends too… I used to actually plan for that, like pick a trail and pack snacks, and now even thinking about texting someone back feels like a whole thing. Mostly I just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more now, not because they did anything, I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. I don’t really notice it happening at the time, but I’ll get to nighttime and realize I basically just had coffee and, like, a few bites of something.\n\nIt’s weird because at the salon I used to snack between clients, or someone would bring muffins or whatever, and now I just kind of forget. Then I’m changing clothes and they feel looser, like my jeans don’t sit the same. So… yeah, I guess I’ve been skipping meals without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. That used to feel good.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends, too. We had this one trail we’d do where someone always forgot water, which was usually me, actually… but now I don’t really look forward to it. I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your sadness is mild or more severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: More severe, I think. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because some days I still get up and do what I’m supposed to do… but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like before I even brush my teeth I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I was planning? I don’t hate any of it. I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really much, no. Like I used to really enjoy talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they were learning something, and going hiking with friends… even just the way the air smells on certain trails, you know, like pine and dirt after rain.\n\nBut now I mostly keep my head down. I avoid people more, not because I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. So if something feels nice, it’s really small and it doesn’t last long.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a long time. Like, I keep trying to think of when it started exactly, because it kind of blended in with everything else, but… about a year and two months, I think.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember around then I kept telling myself it was just a bad week, or maybe I was tired from work, and then weeks turned into months. But yeah… the heavy, flat feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really in the way I used to. I used to look forward to chatting with clients, like hearing about their kids or their vacations, and helping the newer stylists when they were nervous about a cut or color. That used to make me feel useful, I guess.\n\nAnd hiking with friends too… I used to actually plan for that, like pick a trail and pack snacks, and now even thinking about texting someone back feels like a whole thing. Mostly I just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more now, not because they did anything, I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. I don’t really notice it happening at the time, but I’ll get to nighttime and realize I basically just had coffee and, like, a few bites of something.\n\nIt’s weird because at the salon I used to snack between clients, or someone would bring muffins or whatever, and now I just kind of forget. Then I’m changing clothes and they feel looser, like my jeans don’t sit the same. So… yeah, I guess I’ve been skipping meals without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I know I kind of said it before, but… I used to like the salon stuff, like talking with clients, hearing all their stories, or helping the newer girls when they were unsure about something. That used to feel good.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends, too. We had this one trail we’d do where someone always forgot water, which was usually me, actually… but now I don’t really look forward to it. I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your sadness is mild or more severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: More severe, I think. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because some days I still get up and do what I’m supposed to do… but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like before I even brush my teeth I’m already thinking, what’s the point of trying to build the life I was planning? Which sounds awful out loud. I used to have these ideas about where I’d work, maybe having my own chair, my own little space, and now it just feels… far away. So yeah, not mild. More like it’s sitting on me most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have lost interest in most things or just some things?\n\nPatient: Most things, I think… yeah. Like, I can’t really think of much I actually look forward to anymore.\n\nI used to be really into chatting with clients at the salon, you know, like hearing about their kids or their vacations or whatever drama was happening. And I’d help the newer stylists when they got nervous, which made me feel useful. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to get pulled into conversations.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends used to be a whole thing too, like we’d make playlists and stop for snacks after… actually there was this one place with the best fries, but… yeah, even that doesn’t feel fun now. I don’t hate any of it. I just don’t have that spark in me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really much, no. I keep trying to think of something because it sounds so sad to say there’s nothing, but the things I used to enjoy don’t really hit the same.\n\nLike at the salon, I used to love talking with clients, and helping the newer stylists when they got stuck or nervous. There was this one girl who always panicked about bangs—honestly, bangs make everyone panic—but I liked being the person she asked. Now I mostly just keep my head down and try not to get pulled into stuff.\n\nAnd hiking with my friends used to be fun too, but now even that feels like… effort. I avoid people more. It’s not that I hate them. I just don’t have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your sadness is interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, I think it is.\n\nLike work has gotten harder in ways that feel embarrassing because it’s basic stuff, you know? Salon routines I used to do without thinking, now I make mistakes or I’m just slow, and then I can tell my manager doesn’t really trust me the same. Some clients too. They used to ask for me and now I feel like they’re watching me mess up before I even do anything.\n\nAnd I’ve been canceling things I normally handled. Not big dramatic things, just… things people counted on me for. I stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts at the senior center, which makes me feel awful because some of them really looked forward to it. There was this one lady who always wanted her hair curled the exact same way—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nI’ve pulled away from my friends too. I don’t answer as much, I don’t go places. So yeah, it’s not just me feeling sad in my room. It’s messing with my actual life.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your sadness is present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much every day. I mean, maybe it’s not the exact same level all day, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, like this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll be brushing my teeth or getting dressed for work and I’ll think about how I was supposed to be building this whole life, you know? Like getting better at hair, maybe having my own little chair setup that felt like mine, maybe an apartment with plants I don’t kill—sorry, that’s random. But now it just feels like… what’s the point of trying to build any of it. So yeah, it’s there most days.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your sadness is present most of the day or nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say nearly every day. Most mornings I wake up and it’s just already there, this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt kind of follows me around most of the day too, even if I’m doing normal things. Like I’ll be thinking about work, or how I used to picture building this whole life for myself, and then it just turns into this feeling like… what’s the point of trying. I don’t know. It’s hard to explain, but yeah, it’s there a lot.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness?\n\nPatient: Um… about a year and two months, I think. Like, not just a bad week or something.\n\nIt’s strange because I remember thinking last year, “Okay, maybe this is just stress,” and then months kept stacking up. Seasons changed, clients changed their hair colors, my friend kept talking about this one guy she was dating—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But yeah… the heavy, flat sadness has been with me for around fourteen months."
